      "quote": "Keibu Keioiba, also known as Kabui Keioiba, is a mythical creature with the head of a tiger and the body of a human in the Meitei mythology and folklore of Manipur. As punishment of his pride, he could not completely turn back to his original human form.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      "source_id": "keibu-keioiba-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, ia dahulu pendeta Kabui Salang Maiba yang menggunakan sihir untuk berubah menjadi harimau.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keibu-keioiba-c03",
      "source_id": "keibu-keioiba-s1",
      "quote": "Keibu Keioiba was a human in the daytime and a tiger at night. During night, he prowled around looking for food. One night, he found an older woman's house and planned to devour her.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisahnya, seorang perempuan tua mengarahkan makhluk itu kepada Thabaton yang tinggal sendirian.",

      }

      "id": "keibu-keioiba-c04",
      "source_id": "keibu-keioiba-s1",
      "quote": "Shortly after this, Thabaton's brothers return home from work and discover that Thabaton is missing. They ask the old woman about their sister, and she tells them about Keibu Keioiba.       "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membawa Thabaton ke hutan dan memaksanya menjadi istrinya.",
        "en": "He takes Thabaton to the forest and forces her to become his wife."

    },
    {
      "id": "keibu-keioiba-c05",
      "source_id": "keibu-keioiba-s1",
      "quote": "Thabaton gives him a hollow utong (bamboo pipe) open at both ends. She tells Keibu Keioiba to fetch water from the stream with the utong.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mahishi adalah sosok iblis perempuan dalam mitologi Hindu yang kisahnya berkaitan dengan Ayyappa dan Sabarimala. Sumber ini menyebutnya saudari Mahishasura, iblis berwujud kerbau.",
      "en": "Mahishi is a demoness in Hindu mythology whose story concerns Ayyappa and Sabarimala. This source calls her the sister of the buffalo demon Mahishasura.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mahishi-demoness-c01",
        "mahishi-demoness-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia menerima anugerah bahwa hanya anak dari persatuan Shiva dan Vishnu yang dapat membunuhnya. Kisah kelahiran Ayyappan menjawab syarat anugerah itu melalui persatuan Shiva dengan Mohini, penjelmaan perempuan Vishnu. Ayyappan menangkap tanduknya dan menjatuhkannya ke bumi dalam pertarungan. Kerala Tourism menjelaskan bahwa misi kelahiran Ayyappa adalah menghapus ancaman Mahishi.",
      "en": "She receives a boon that only a child of Shiva and Vishnu's union can kill her. Ayyappan's birth satisfies that condition through Shiva's union with Mohini, Vishnu's female manifestation. Ayyappan seizes her horns and casts her to Earth during their battle. Kerala Tourism explains Ayyappa's birth mission as eliminating the threat posed by Mahishi.",

        "mahishi-demoness-c04",
        "mahishi-demoness-c05",
        "mahishi-demoness-c06"
      ]

      "source_id": "mahishi-demoness-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu mythology, Mahishi is a powerful demoness whose narrative is central to the origin of Lord Ayyappa and the traditions of the Sabarimala temple. She was the sister of the buffalo-demon Mahishasura.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

    },
    {
      "id": "mahishi-demoness-c02",
      "source_id": "mahishi-demoness-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu mythology, Mahishi is a powerful demoness whose narrative is central to the origin of Lord Ayyappa and the traditions of the Sabarimala temple. She was the sister of the buffalo-demon Mahishasura.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber ini menyebutnya saudari Mahishasura, iblis berwujud kerbau.",
        "en": "This source calls her the sister of the buffalo demon Mahishasura."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahishi-demoness-c03",
      "source_id": "mahishi-demoness-s1",
      "quote": "From Brahma, she secured a specific boon: that she could only be slain by a child born of the union of Lord Shiva and Lord Vishnu. Believing such a birth to be biologically impossible, she unleashed a reign of terror, conquering the Svarga and ousting Indra from his throne.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menerima anugerah bahwa hanya anak dari persatuan Shiva dan Vishnu yang dapat membunuhnya.",
        "en": "She receives a boon that only a child of Shiva and Vishnu's union can kill her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahishi-demoness-c04",
      "source_id": "mahishi-demoness-s1",
      "quote": "To counter Mahishi's invincibility, the deities sought a solution. Following the Samudra Manthana , Shiva was captivated by the beauty of Mohini, the female avatar of Vishnu. Their union led to the birth of Ayyappan (also known as Manikandan or Dharma Sastha), the divine child born of two male deities, thereby fulfilling the technical requirements of Mahishi’s boon.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah kelahiran Ayyappan menjawab syarat anugerah itu melalui persatuan Shiva dengan Mohini, penjelmaan perempuan Vishnu.",
        "en": "Ayyappan's birth satisfies that condition through Shiva's union with Mohini, Vishnu's female manifestation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahishi-demoness-c05",
      "source_id": "mahishi-demoness-s1",
      "quote": "When Mahishi attempted to storm heaven once more, Ayyappan, then a young prince, ascended to the celestial realms. He engaged the demoness in a fierce battle, eventually catching her by her horns and hurling her down to Earth. She landed on the banks of the Azhuthayar River near Sabarimala, where Ayyappan performed a divine dance on her body as she died.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ayyappan menangkap tanduknya dan menjatuhkannya ke bumi dalam pertarungan.",
        "en": "Ayyappan seizes her horns and casts her to Earth during their battle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahishi-demoness-c06",
      "source_id": "mahishi-demoness-s2",
      "quote": "The birth mission of Ayyappa was to eliminate the threat caused by Mahishi, a demoness.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kerala Tourism menjelaskan bahwa misi kelahiran Ayyappa adalah menghapus ancaman Mahishi.",
        "en": "Kerala Tourism explains Ayyappa's birth mission as eliminating the threat posed by Mahishi."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Naga memiliki peran dalam cerita, sastra, mitologi, dan agama Meitei di Manipur. Khelchandra Singh menggambarkan Pakhangba sebagai raja-dewa Manipur dalam kepercayaan setempat.",

        "meitei-dragons-c01",
        "meitei-dragons-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam penuturan itu, ia dapat bergerak sebagai naga pada malam hari. Raja-raja Manipur menggunakan citra naga sebagai lambang kerajaan.",

      "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Munjya adalah hantu anak laki-laki yang menurut cerita rakyat Maharashtra dan pesisir Konkan meninggal setelah upacara benang suci tetapi sebelum menikah. Mereka dipercaya menghuni pohon peepal.",

      "claim_ids": [
        "munjya-c01",
        "munjya-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagian cerita menggambarkan hantu bujang yang gelisah, cerdik, dan menguasai beberapa bahasa. Meskipun umumnya tidak berbahaya, mereka dapat membalas orang yang mengotori pohon tempat tinggalnya. Cerita tertentu menyebut pelemparan batu atau kecelakaan sebagai bentuk pembalasan.",

      ]

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Poubi Lai adalah naga piton dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Meitei yang menghuni Danau Loktak di Manipur.",
    "en": "Poubi Lai is a dragon python of Meitei mythology and folklore dwelling in Manipur's Loktak Lake.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "poubi-lai-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Poubi Lai adalah naga piton dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Meitei yang menghuni Danau Loktak di Manipur. Dalam satu kisah, ia marah akibat kegiatan nelayan Moirang dan mengancam kerajaan.",
      "en": "Poubi Lai is a dragon python of Meitei mythology and folklore dwelling in Manipur's Loktak Lake. In one tale fishing by Moirang's fishermen angers it and it threatens the kingdom.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "poubi-lai-c01",
        "poubi-lai-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia menuntut satu keranjang beras dan satu manusia setiap hari dari raja Moirang. Versi lain menempatkannya di Karang Hill dan menggambarkannya memangsa nelayan. Sumber menyebut Poubi Lai dalam naskah Meitei Khongul Lirakpa.",
      "en": "It demands a basket of rice and a human each day from Moirang's king. Another version locates it at Karang Hill and describes it preying on fishermen. The source records Poubi Lai in the Meitei manuscript Khongul Lirakpa.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "poubi-lai-c03",
        "poubi-lai-c04",
        "poubi-lai-c05"
      ]

  "claims": [

      "id": "poubi-lai-c01",
      "source_id": "poubi-lai-s1",
      "quote": "Poubi Lai was an ancient dragon python, who dwelled in the Loktak Lake of Manipur, in Meitei mythology and folklore. It is also referred to as \"Loch Ness Monster of Manipur\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Poubi Lai adalah naga piton dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Meitei yang menghuni Danau Loktak di Manipur.",
        "en": "Poubi Lai is a dragon python of Meitei mythology and folklore dwelling in Manipur's Loktak Lake."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "poubi-lai-c02",
      "source_id": "poubi-lai-s1",
      "quote": "In the Loktak lake, the spirit of Poubi Lai was awakened by the fishing activities of the fishermen of Moirang. Being angered, Poubi Lai destroyed many human habitats and killed many people.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu kisah, ia marah akibat kegiatan nelayan Moirang dan mengancam kerajaan.",
        "en": "In one tale fishing by Moirang's fishermen angers it and it threatens the kingdom."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "poubi-lai-c03",
      "source_id": "poubi-lai-s1",
      "quote": "He threatened the King of Ancient Moirang to offer him one Shangbai (basket) of rice and one human every day.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menuntut satu keranjang beras dan satu manusia setiap hari dari raja Moirang.",
        "en": "It demands a basket of rice and a human each day from Moirang's king."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "poubi-lai-c04",
      "source_id": "poubi-lai-s1",
      "quote": "In another version of the story, Poubi Lai (Paobirai) lives in the isle of the Karang Hill in the Loktak lake. It hunted the fishermen of Moirang coming to the lake and devoured them. It even attacked human settlement and hunted for its prey.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain menempatkannya di Karang Hill dan menggambarkannya memangsa nelayan.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "poubi-lai-c05",
      "source_id": "poubi-lai-s1",
      "quote": "Poubi Lai (Paobirai) is mentioned in the ancient Meitei manuscript called the \"Khongul Lirakpa\".",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

      "quote": "The buru or bura is an aquatic cryptic animal featured in a foundation myth of the Apatani people. Stories of the migration of the Apatani to their present location in the Ziro Valley of Arunachal Pradesh tell of a swamp occupying the valley, inhabited by fierce, crocodile-like creatures.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah migrasi menempatkannya di rawa Lembah Ziro sebelum kawasan itu dikeringkan untuk sawah.",
        "en": "Migration stories place it in the Ziro Valley swamp before the land was drained for rice fields."
      }

      "id": "buru-legendary-creature-c03",
      "source_id": "buru-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "In 1945 and 1946, James Philip Mills and Charles Stonor gathered details about the buru from the Apatani people. According to the Apatani elders, when their forefathers migrated to Ziro Valley, the valley was primarily a marsh which was populated by burus. The Apatani people decided to settle in the valley because of its fertility and good climate.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mills dan Stonor mengumpulkan cerita tentang buru dari masyarakat Apatani pada 1945 dan 1946.",
        "en": "Mills and Stonor collected buru accounts from Apatani people in 1945 and 1946."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "buru-legendary-creature-c04",
      "source_id": "buru-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "The burus were ostensibly eliminated when the Apatani people drained the wetlands to facilitate rice cultivation. Most of the burus died because of the drainage, and many supposedly went underground into the springs.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tetua Apatani menuturkan bahwa pengeringan rawa menghilangkan sebagian besar buru, sementara sebagian masuk ke mata air bawah tanah.",
        "en": "Apatani elders recount that draining the wetlands killed most burus while some went underground into springs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "buru-legendary-creature-c05",
      "source_id": "buru-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "The last buru was said to be reported by a young woman, who sighted it in a spring one night while she was drawing water. The startled lady told her father about the incident. The next day the whole village helped fill the spring with stones and clay.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah penampakan terakhir menyebut seorang perempuan yang melihat buru di mata air, kemudian warga menutup mata air itu.",
        "en": "The last-sighting tale describes a woman seeing a buru in a spring, which villagers then filled in."
      }
    }

      "id": "ichchhadhari-nag-c02",
      "source_id": "ichchhadhari-nag-s1",
      "quote": "ಇಚ್ಛಧಾರಿ ನಾಗ್ (ಸ್ತ್ರೀಲಿಂಗ: ಇಚ್ಛಧಾರಿ ನಾಗಿನ್) ಭಾರತೀಯ ಜಾನಪದ ಮತ್ತು ಪೌರಾಣಿಕೆ ಕಥೆಗಳಲ್ಲಿ ಬರುವ  ಆಕಾರವನ್ನು ಬದಲಾಯಿಸುವ ನಾಗರಹಾವುಗಳಾಗಿವೆ. ಇವುಗಳು ಹಾವಿನಿಂದ ಮನುಷ್ಯರೂಪಕ್ಕೆ ಮತ್ತು ಮನುಷ್ಯರೂಪದಿಂದ ಪುನಃ ಹಾವಾಗುವ ಶಕ್ತಿ ಹೊಂದಿವೆ ಎಂಬ ಕಥೆಗಳಿವೆ. ಅವರು ಶಿವನ ಮಹಾನ್ ಭಕ್ತರಾಗಿದ್ದಾರೆ.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita, mereka dapat berubah antara bentuk ular dan manusia.",
        "en": "In stories they can change between serpent and human form."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichchhadhari-nag-c03",
      "source_id": "ichchhadhari-nag-s1",
      "quote": "ಕತೆಗಳಲ್ಲಿರುವಂತೆ ಸಾಮಾನ್ಯ ಗಂಡು ನಾಗರಹಾವು ಇಚ್ಚಾಧಾರಿ ನಾಗ (ಗಂಡು ಆಕಾರವನ್ನು ಬದಲಾಯಿಸುವ ನಾಗರಹಾವು) ಆಗುತ್ತದೆ ಮತ್ತು ಸಾಮಾನ್ಯ ಹೆಣ್ಣು ನಾಗರಹಾವು ೧೦೦ ವರ್ಷಗಳ ತಪಸ್ಸಿನ ನಂತರ ಇಚ್ಚಾಧಾರಿ ನಾಗಿನ್ (ಹೆಣ್ಣು ಆಕಾರವನ್ನು ಬದಲಾಯಿಸಿದ ನಾಗರಹಾವು ಆಗುತ್ತವೆ. ಶಿವನಿಂದ ಆಶೀರ್ವದಿಸಲ್ಪಟ್ಟ ನಂತರ ಅವರು ತಮ್ಮದೇ ಆದ ಮಾನವ ರೂಪವನ್ನು ಪಡೆಯುತ್ತಾರೆ. ಜೊತೆಗೆ ಇವು ಯಾವುದೇ ಜೀವಿಯ ಆಕಾರವನ್ನು ಹೊಂದುವ ಸಾಮರ್ಥ್ಯವನ್ನು ಪಡೆಯುತ್ತಾರೆ.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dipercaya memperoleh wujud manusia setelah mendapat berkat Shiva.",
        "en": "They are believed to gain human form after Shiva's blessing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichchhadhari-nag-c04",
      "source_id": "ichchhadhari-nag-s1",
      "quote": "ಇಚ್ಛಧಾರಿ ನಾಗಗಳು ಮತ್ತು ನಾಗಿಣಿಯರು ನಾಗಮಣಿ ಎಂಬ ಆಭರಣವನ್ನು ಹೊಂದಿರುತ್ತಾರೆ. ಇವು ಯಾವುದೇ ಅಮೂಲ್ಯವಾದ ಕಲ್ಲುಗಳಿಗಿಂತ ಹೆಚ್ಚು ಮೌಲ್ಯಯುತವಾಗಿದೆ. ಏಕೆಂದರೆ ಅದು ತನ್ನ ಮಾಲೀಕರನ್ನು ಮತ್ತೆ ಬದುಕಿಸುವ ಮತ್ತು ಅಪಾರವಾದ ಶಕ್ತಿಯನ್ನು ನೀಡುವ  ಶಕ್ತಿಯನ್ನು ಹೊಂದಿದೆ. ಈ ನಾಗಮಣಿಯನ್ನು ಕದಿಯಲು ಪ್ರಯತ್ನಿಸಿದಾಗ ಆ ಜನರು ಹಾವು ಕಡಿತದಿಂದ ಸಾಯುತ್ತಾರೆ ಎಂದು ದಂತಕಥೆಗಳು ಹೇಳುತ್ತವೆ.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerita menghubungkan mereka dengan permata Nagamani yang dijaga dari pencuri.",
        "en": "Stories connect them with a Nagamani jewel guarded against thieves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichchhadhari-nag-c05",
      "source_id": "ichchhadhari-nag-s1",
      "quote": "ಸಪೇರಾ (ಸ್ನೇಕ್ ಚಾರ್ಮರ್) ಅಥವಾ ಹಾವಾಡಿಗ ಬಳಸುವ ಗಾಳಿ ವಾದ್ಯವಾದ ಪುಂಗಿಯ ಶಬ್ದವನ್ನು ಕೇಳಿದಾಗ ನಾಗ್ಗಳು ಮತ್ತು ನಾಗಿನ್ನರು/ನಾಗಿಣಿಯರು ತಮ್ಮ ನಿಯಂತ್ರಣವನ್ನು ಕಳೆದುಕೊಳ್ಳುತ್ತಾರೆ ಮತ್ತು ತಮ್ಮ ನಿಜವಾದ ರೂಪವನ್ನು ತೋರಿಸುತ್ತಾರೆ ಎಂನ ನಂಬಿಕೆಯಿದೆ.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bunyi alat pungi dipercaya membuat mereka kehilangan kendali dan menampakkan wujud asli.",
        "en": "The pungi instrument's sound is believed to make them lose control and reveal their true form."
      }
    }
  ],

      "en": "Pinglak is a lion character in the Panchatantra fables. His tale belongs to the Mitrabheda section about a lion and bull's broken friendship.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pinglak-c01",
        "pinglak-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sanjeewak adalah banteng yang ditinggalkan majikannya di hutan ketika sakit. Dua penasihatnya adalah jakal Karkat dan Damnak. Damanaka merusak persahabatan Pingalaka dan Sanjivaka karena iri.",
      "en": "Sanjeewak is a bull abandoned by his master in a forest when ill. His two advisers are the jackals Karkat and Damnak. Damanaka breaks up Pingalaka and Sanjivaka's friendship out of jealousy.",

It is hypothesis and the story is used to compare the real moral and relevant at present also. Panchatantra, a collection of stories which depict animals in human situations (see anthropomorphism, Talking animals in fiction).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pinglak adalah tokoh singa dalam kumpulan fabel Panchatantra.",
        "en": "Pinglak is a lion character in the Panchatantra fables."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pinglak-c02",
      "source_id": "pinglak-s1",
      "quote": "The first chapter is termed \"Mitrabheda\", which means betrayal of friends. This story is about a lion and a bull who became friends. Somehow, a dispute occurred between them and they both fought. Finally, one of them died.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisahnya berada dalam bagian Mitrabheda tentang rusaknya persahabatan singa dan banteng.",
        "en": "His tale belongs to the Mitrabheda section about a lion and bull's broken friendship."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pinglak-c03",
      "source_id": "pinglak-s1",
      "quote": "Sanjeewak was a bull who, because of his incapabilities and illness was discarded by his master and was left alone in a jungle, where he sat on the bank of Yamuna river and grew stronger.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sanjeewak adalah banteng yang ditinggalkan majikannya di hutan ketika sakit.",
        "en": "Sanjeewak is a bull abandoned by his master in a forest when ill."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pinglak-c04",
      "source_id": "pinglak-s1",
      "quote": "The lion had two cunning jackal ministers named Karkat and Damnak.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dua penasihatnya adalah jakal Karkat dan Damnak.",
        "en": "His two advisers are the jackals Karkat and Damnak."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pinglak-c05",
      "source_id": "pinglak-s1",
      "quote": "Its original Indian version is Mitra-bheda, The Separation of Friends. In the first story, a friendship arises between the lion Piṅgalaka, the king of the forest, and Sañjīvaka, a bull. Karataka ('Horribly Howling') and Damanaka ('Victor') are two jackals that are retainers to the lion king.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Damanaka merusak persahabatan Pingalaka dan Sanjivaka karena iri.",
        "en": "Damanaka breaks up Pingalaka and Sanjivaka's friendship out of jealousy."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Sanbo Kojin adalah dewa api, perapian, dan dapur dalam tradisi Jepang. Kojin mewakili api yang dikendalikan dan digunakan untuk tujuan baik.",
      "en": "Sanbo Kojin is a god of fire, the hearth and the kitchen in Japanese tradition. Kojin embodies fire controlled and used for a good purpose.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sanbo-kojin-c01",
        "sanbo-kojin-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dipercaya menghancurkan ketidakmurnian dan mengawasi rumah tangga. Ia kadang diidentifikasi dengan Fudo Myo-o. Representasinya secara tradisional ditempatkan di dekat perapian. JAANUS menyebut Kojin sebagai dewa yang melindungi dari bencana dan membawa keberuntungan.",
      "en": "He is believed to destroy impurity and watch over the household. He is sometimes identified with Fudo Myo-o. His representation is traditionally placed near the hearth. JAANUS describes Kojin as a god protecting against disaster and bringing good fortune.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "sanbo-kojin-c01",
      "source_id": "sanbo-kojin-s1",
      "quote": "Kōjin, also known as Sambō-Kōjin or Sanbō-Kōjin (三宝荒神), is the Japanese kami (god) of fire, the hearth and the kitchen.  He is sometimes called Kamado-gami (竃神), literally the god of the stove. He represents violent forces that are turned toward the betterment of humankind.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sanbo Kojin adalah dewa api, perapian, dan dapur dalam tradisi Jepang.",
        "en": "Sanbo Kojin is a god of fire, the hearth and the kitchen in Japanese tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanbo-kojin-c02",
      "source_id": "sanbo-kojin-s1",
      "quote": "However, Kōjin embodies fire controlled and turned toward a good purpose.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kojin mewakili api yang dikendalikan dan digunakan untuk tujuan baik.",
        "en": "Kojin embodies fire controlled and used for a good purpose."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanbo-kojin-c03",
      "source_id": "sanbo-kojin-s1",
      "quote": "He is said to destroy all impurity. He is also responsible for watching over the household and reporting any misdeeds to the kami of the village or city.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dipercaya menghancurkan ketidakmurnian dan mengawasi rumah tangga.",
        "en": "He is believed to destroy impurity and watch over the household."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanbo-kojin-c04",
      "source_id": "sanbo-kojin-s1",
      "quote": "Kōjin is sometimes identified as an incarnation of Fudō Myō-Ō, who is likewise depicted as surrounded by flames and tasked with dealing with misdeeds.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia kadang diidentifikasi dengan Fudo Myo-o.",
        "en": "He is sometimes identified with Fudo Myo-o."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanbo-kojin-c05",
      "source_id": "sanbo-kojin-s1",
      "quote": "Traditionally, a representation of Kōjin is placed near the hearth.  This representation might be a simple fuda (memorial tablet) in many homes, or it might be as elaborate as a statue, as is common in Buddhist temples.  In his statues, Kōjin is depicted with flaming hair, fangs, and a contorted face, and he often wields a bow and arrows.  He has two pairs of hands.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Representasinya secara tradisional ditempatkan di dekat perapian.",
        "en": "His representation is traditionally placed near the hearth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sanbo-kojin-c06",
      "source_id": "sanbo-kojin-s2",
      "quote": "A god that protects against disaster and brings good fortune.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

      "en": "Banjhakri and Banjhakrini are shamanic deities in Kirati tradition in Nepal and the Indian areas named by the source. They are a couple understood as supernatural shamans of the forest.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "banjhakri-and-banjhakrini-c01",
        "banjhakri-and-banjhakrini-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Banjhakri digambarkan bertubuh pendek, berbulu, bertelinga besar, dan berkaki terbalik. Ia membawa anak berpotensi menjadi dukun ke guanya untuk pelatihan. Anak yang lolos inisiasi Banjhakrini kembali dengan kemampuan perdukunan.",

      "quote": "Chedipe (Hindi:  चेदिप, literally \"prostitute\") is a witch-vampire in the folklore of the region around the Godavari River in India. They are associated with the betals,.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

        "en": "Tales say women dying unnaturally may become chedipes."
      }

    {
      "id": "chedipe-c03",
      "source_id": "chedipe-s1",
      "quote": "The chedipe is described as a vulgar woman riding on a tiger in the moonlit night.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",

        "susna-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Susna dikaitkan dengan kekeringan dan akar kata Sus yang berarti mengering. Ia dikaitkan dengan Vritra, asura lain yang menghalangi sungai. Dalam penuturan Rigveda, Indra menghancurkan benteng Susna dan mengirim hujan atas permintaan Kutsa.",
      "en": "The name Susna is connected with drought and the root Sus meaning to dry up. He is associated with Vritra, another asura obstructing rivers. In the Rigveda account Indra destroys Susna's fortress and sends rain at Kutsa's request.",

    {
      "id": "susna-c03",
      "source_id": "susna-s1",
      "quote": "Susna means \"drought\" from the root Sus, which translates to \"dry up\". The similar \"sosna\" is an old Slavic word for the pine tree.",

      "context": "etymology",

    {
      "id": "susna-c04",
      "source_id": "susna-s1",
      "quote": "In Hinduism, Susna is an asura commonly associated with drought, famine, and hoarding. An enemy of Indra, the asura makes multiple appearances across a number of Vedic texts. He is often associated with Vritra, another asura which obstructs the rivers of the world.",

    {
      "id": "susna-c05",
      "source_id": "susna-s1",
      "quote": "To defeat the asura, Indra destroys Susna's fortress, and, at the request of his follower Kutsa, sends rains to end the drought, defeating the asura.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ucchusma adalah Vidyaraja dalam Buddhisme Mahayana dan Vajrayana.",
    "en": "Ucchusma is a Vidyaraja in Mahayana and Vajrayana Buddhism.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ucchusma-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ucchusma adalah Vidyaraja dalam Buddhisme Mahayana dan Vajrayana. Tradisi Buddhis China dan Jepang mengenalnya melalui beragam nama dan julukan.",
      "en": "Ucchusma is a Vidyaraja in Mahayana and Vajrayana Buddhism. Chinese and Japanese Buddhist traditions know him by several names and epithets.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ucchusma-c01",
        "ucchusma-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Surangama Sutra menempatkannya di antara sosok yang menjelaskan cara memahami kebenaran tertinggi. Ucchusma Vajrapala Sutra menyebutnya manifestasi Vajra dari Shakyamuni Buddha. Dalam kisah itu, ia mengubah kekuatan pencemar Raja Brahma menjadi tanah biasa. Teks Zhu tian zhuan menyebut Wuchusemo sebagai kepala api yang bersumpah melawan para pengganggu ajaran.",
      "en": "The Surangama Sutra includes him among figures explaining their methods for understanding ultimate truth. The Ucchusma Vajrapala Sutra identifies him as Shakyamuni Buddha's Vajra manifestation. In that tale he turns the Brahma King's contaminating forces into ordinary soil. The Zhu tian zhuan calls Wuchusemo the fire head who vows to oppose enemies of the teaching.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ucchusma-c03",
        "ucchusma-c04",
        "ucchusma-c05",
        "ucchusma-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ucchusma-c01",
      "source_id": "ucchusma-s1",
      "quote": "Ucchuṣma (Chinese: 穢跡金剛; pinyin: Huìjì Jīngāng; Rōmaji: Eshaku Kongō) is a Vidyārāja in Mahayana and Vajrayana Buddhism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ucchusma adalah Vidyaraja dalam Buddhisme Mahayana dan Vajrayana.",
        "en": "Ucchusma is a Vidyaraja in Mahayana and Vajrayana Buddhism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ucchusma-c02",
      "source_id": "ucchusma-s1",
      "quote": "Ucchuṣma is known by various epithets and names in Chinese and Japanese Buddhism, which developed due to controversy surrounding the negative associations with impurities surrounding some of his names.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Buddhis China dan Jepang mengenalnya melalui beragam nama dan julukan.",
        "en": "Chinese and Japanese Buddhist traditions know him by several names and epithets."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ucchusma-c03",
      "source_id": "ucchusma-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Śūraṅgama Sūtra, Shakyamuni Buddha asked the bodhisattvas and arhats to present their methods of understanding the ultimate truth. The eighteenth person to present his character was Ucchuṣma. The Sūtra states:",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Surangama Sutra menempatkannya di antara sosok yang menjelaskan cara memahami kebenaran tertinggi.",
        "en": "The Surangama Sutra includes him among figures explaining their methods for understanding ultimate truth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ucchusma-c04",
      "source_id": "ucchusma-s1",
      "quote": "The Ucchuṣma Vajrapāla Sūtra asserts that Ucchuṣma is actually the Vajra manifestation of Shakyamuni Buddha.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ucchusma Vajrapala Sutra menyebutnya manifestasi Vajra dari Shakyamuni Buddha.",
        "en": "The Ucchusma Vajrapala Sutra identifies him as Shakyamuni Buddha's Vajra manifestation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ucchusma-c05",
      "source_id": "ucchusma-s1",
      "quote": "Despite the defiled energy hurled at Him by the Brahma King, Ucchuṣma was unharmed as he immediately turned these forces of contamination into ordinary soil.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah itu, ia mengubah kekuatan pencemar Raja Brahma menjadi tanah biasa.",
        "en": "In that tale he turns the Brahma King's contaminating forces into ordinary soil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ucchusma-c06",
      "source_id": "ucchusma-s2",
      "quote": "楞严中本名乌刍瑟摩。此云火头。经云。我以火光三昧力故。成阿罗汉。心发大愿。诸佛成道。我为力士。亲伏魔怨。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 11",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Zhu tian zhuan menyebut Wuchusemo sebagai kepala api yang bersumpah melawan para pengganggu ajaran.",
        "en": "The Zhu tian zhuan calls Wuchusemo the fire head who vows to oppose enemies of the teaching."
      }
    }
  ],

      "claim_ids": [
        "diting-c01",
        "diting-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Salah satu legenda menyebutnya berasal dari anjing putih. Anjing yang menyertai Kim Gyo-gak dipercaya menjadi Diting setelah Ksitigarbha tercerahkan. Dalam Journey to the West, Diting dapat membedakan Sun Wukong asli dari tiruannya.",
      "en": "One legend identifies its origin as a white dog. The dog accompanying Kim Gyo-gak is believed to become Diting after Ksitigarbha's enlightenment. In Journey to the West Diting can distinguish the true Sun Wukong from the impostor.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Eight Great Yaksa Generals adalah para dewa penjaga dalam Buddhisme.",
    "en": "The Eight Great Yaksa Generals are guardian deities in Buddhism.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "eight-great-yaksa-generals-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Eight Great Yaksa Generals adalah para dewa penjaga dalam Buddhisme. Mereka adalah pengikut Vaisravana, penjaga utara dan raja yaksa.",
      "en": "The Eight Great Yaksa Generals are guardian deities in Buddhism. They are retainers of Vaisravana, guardian of the north and king of yakshas.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "eight-great-yaksa-generals-c01",
        "eight-great-yaksa-generals-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Asia Timur mereka juga dikenal sebagai Eight Great Heavenly Kings. Narasi pertobatan mengubah roh yang dahulu jahat menjadi dewa penjaga yang baik. Para jenderal itu ditempatkan pada tingkat tertinggi di antara yaksa yang melayani Vaisravana.",
      "en": "In East Asia they are also known as the Eight Great Heavenly Kings. A conversion narrative transforms formerly malevolent spirits into benevolent guardians. The generals rank at the top among yakshas serving Vaisravana.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "eight-great-yaksa-generals-c01",
      "source_id": "eight-great-yaksa-generals-s1",
      "quote": "The Eight Great Yakṣa Generals (Chinese: 八大夜叉大将; pinyin: Bādà Yèchā Dàjiàng), or simply the Eight Yakṣa Generals, are guardian deities in Buddhism. They are retainers of Vaiśravaṇa, guardian of the north and king of the yakṣas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Eight Great Yaksa Generals adalah para dewa penjaga dalam Buddhisme.",
        "en": "The Eight Great Yaksa Generals are guardian deities in Buddhism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "eight-great-yaksa-generals-c02",
      "source_id": "eight-great-yaksa-generals-s1",
      "quote": "They are retainers of Vaiśravaṇa, guardian of the north and king of the yakṣas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka adalah pengikut Vaisravana, penjaga utara dan raja yaksa.",
        "en": "They are retainers of Vaisravana, guardian of the north and king of yakshas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "eight-great-yaksa-generals-c03",
      "source_id": "eight-great-yaksa-generals-s1",
      "quote": "In East Asia, they are also variously known as the Eight Great Yakṣas (八大藥叉), the Eight Great Heavenly Kings (八大天王), and the Eight Brothers of Vaiśravaṇa (毘沙門八兄弟).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Asia Timur mereka juga dikenal sebagai Eight Great Heavenly Kings.",
        "en": "In East Asia they are also known as the Eight Great Heavenly Kings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "eight-great-yaksa-generals-c04",
      "source_id": "eight-great-yaksa-generals-s1",
      "quote": "Originally malevolent beings, their conversion led to their deification as benevolent guardian deities.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Narasi pertobatan mengubah roh yang dahulu jahat menjadi dewa penjaga yang baik.",
        "en": "A conversion narrative transforms formerly malevolent spirits into benevolent guardians."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "eight-great-yaksa-generals-c05",
      "source_id": "eight-great-yaksa-generals-s1",
      "quote": "Among the many yakṣas under Vaiśravaṇa's rule, the Eight Great Yakṣa Generals are ranked at the top of the hierarchy. Always at Vaiśravaṇa's command, these deities command 36,000 yakṣas that serve their king and are said to protect those who venerate them. A similar list may be found among the Twenty-Eight Great Yakṣa Generals.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para jenderal itu ditempatkan pada tingkat tertinggi di antara yaksa yang melayani Vaisravana.",
        "en": "The generals rank at the top among yakshas serving Vaisravana."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Haosi Namoinu adalah tokoh cerita rakyat Meitei yang berubah menjadi serangga setelah disiksa ibu tiri.",
    "en": "Haosi Namoinu is a Meitei folktale figure who becomes an insect after her stepmother mistreats her.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Haosi Namoinu adalah tokoh cerita rakyat Meitei yang berubah menjadi serangga setelah disiksa ibu tiri. Bentuk serangganya berbeda menurut versi, berupa tonggeret atau jangkrik.",
      "en": "Haosi Namoinu is a Meitei folktale figure who becomes an insect after her stepmother mistreats her. Her insect form varies by version between cicada and cricket.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "haosi-namoinu-c01",
        "haosi-namoinu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia adalah putri Pokmabi Laoshigam Chanu dan Senbi Loikenba. Ayahnya menikah kembali dengan saudari ibunya setelah sang ibu meninggal. Ibu tirinya memaksa ia mengupas padi, memasak, dan menyajikan makanan. Kisahnya disebut dalam Khongjomnubi Nongarol.",
      "en": "She is the daughter of Pokmabi Laoshigam Chanu and Senbi Loikenba. Her father remarries her mother's sister after her mother dies. Her stepmother forces her to husk rice, cook and serve food. Her story appears in the Khongjomnubi Nongarol.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "haosi-namoinu-c03",
        "haosi-namoinu-c04",
        "haosi-namoinu-c05",

      "source_id": "haosi-namoinu-s1",
      "quote": "Haosi Namoinu is a character in Meitei mythology and folklore of Kangleipak in India. After being mistreated by her stepmother, she transforms into a cicada (or cricket, depending on the version) and flies away from the human world.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Haosi Namoinu adalah tokoh cerita rakyat Meitei yang berubah menjadi serangga setelah disiksa ibu tiri.",
        "en": "Haosi Namoinu is a Meitei folktale figure who becomes an insect after her stepmother mistreats her."

      "id": "haosi-namoinu-c02",
      "source_id": "haosi-namoinu-s1",
      "quote": "she transforms into a cicada (or cricket, depending on the version) and flies away from the human world.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk serangganya berbeda menurut versi, berupa tonggeret atau jangkrik.",
        "en": "Her insect form varies by version between cicada and cricket."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haosi-namoinu-c03",
      "source_id": "haosi-namoinu-s1",
      "quote": "She was the only daughter of Lady Pokmabi Laoshigam Chanu and Lord Senbi Loikenba. Her father was a minister of Ancient Moirang, in charge of collecting tributes from the land of Kabo (present day Western Myanmar).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah putri Pokmabi Laoshigam Chanu dan Senbi Loikenba.",
        "en": "She is the daughter of Pokmabi Laoshigam Chanu and Senbi Loikenba."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haosi-namoinu-c04",
      "source_id": "haosi-namoinu-s1",
      "quote": "After Haosi Namoinu's mother's untimely death, her father remarried to Nganurol Laoshigam Chanu (Loisingam Chanu), her mother's sister.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ayahnya menikah kembali dengan saudari ibunya setelah sang ibu meninggal.",
        "en": "Her father remarries her mother's sister after her mother dies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "haosi-namoinu-c05",
      "source_id": "haosi-namoinu-s1",
      "quote": "She was forced to husk rice, cook food, and serve it.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ibu tirinya memaksa ia mengupas padi, memasak, dan menyajikan makanan.",
        "en": "Her stepmother forces her to husk rice, cook and serve food."
      }

      "id": "haosi-namoinu-c06",
      "source_id": "haosi-namoinu-s1",
      "quote": "The story of lady Haosi Namoinu is mentioned  in the Khongjomnubi Nongarol as one of its two content stories. The following is a passage from the text:",

      "statement": {

      "en": "Some tales describe transformation when an animal passes over an unattended corpse. The being is said to resemble the deceased.       "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "hiyang-athouba-c06",
      "source_id": "hiyang-athouba-s1",
      "quote": "When he calls for his wife, the Hiyangthau responds using her voice and invites him inside.",

    "en": "The Khoirentak tiger is a monstrous tiger in Meitei mythology eventually killed by Khuman Khamba.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Khoirentak tiger adalah harimau mengerikan dalam mitologi Meitei yang akhirnya dibunuh Khuman Khamba. Harimau itu dikenal sebagai pemangsa manusia di daerah Khoirentak.",
      "en": "The Khoirentak tiger is a monstrous tiger in Meitei mythology eventually killed by Khuman Khamba. The tiger is known as a man-eater in the Khoirentak area.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "khoirentak-tiger-c01",
        "khoirentak-tiger-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia tinggal di hutan dan menyerang penduduk desa. Salah satu korbannya adalah Kumja Lembi yang dikirim ibu tirinya mencari tanaman di hutan. Angom Nongban Kongyamba meninggal akibat luka yang disebabkan harimau.",
      "en": "It lives in the forests and attacks villagers. One victim is Kumja Lembi, sent by her stepmother to collect herbs in the forest. Angom Nongban Kongyamba dies of wounds caused by the tiger.",

        "khoirentak-tiger-c04",

  "cultural_context": null,

  "claims": [

      "id": "khoirentak-tiger-c01",
      "source_id": "khoirentak-tiger-s1",
      "quote": "The Khoirentak tiger (Meitei: ꯈꯣꯢꯔꯦꯟꯇꯥꯛ ꯀꯤ ꯀꯩꯔꯦꯜ) was a vicious monster in Meitei mythology that lived in Khoirentak. It was eventually killed by Khuman Khamba.\nThe tiger caused havoc to the people of the Khoirentak in Ancient Moirang, thereby becoming the \"man eater\" of the region. Finally, Khuman Khamba and Nongban were ordered by the king, to catch the beast.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Khoirentak tiger adalah harimau mengerikan dalam mitologi Meitei yang akhirnya dibunuh Khuman Khamba.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khoirentak-tiger-c02",
      "source_id": "khoirentak-tiger-s1",
      "quote": "The tiger caused havoc to the people of the Khoirentak in Ancient Moirang, thereby becoming the \"man eater\" of the region.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Harimau itu dikenal sebagai pemangsa manusia di daerah Khoirentak.",
        "en": "The tiger is known as a man-eater in the Khoirentak area."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khoirentak-tiger-c03",
      "source_id": "khoirentak-tiger-s1",
      "quote": "The tiger lived in the forests of the Khoirentak region and attacked villagers. It became a serious threat to the people and was responsible for several deaths. The events related to the tiger later became part of a major conflict involving royal figures of Moirang.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tinggal di hutan dan menyerang penduduk desa.",
        "en": "It lives in the forests and attacks villagers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khoirentak-tiger-c04",
      "source_id": "khoirentak-tiger-s1",
      "quote": "One notable victim was Kumja Lembi, also known as Kumcha Lempi. She was a poor girl who lived with her stepmother and father. Her father was often away from home because of work. Her stepmother sent her to the forest to collect herbs. During this task, she was killed by the tiger.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu korbannya adalah Kumja Lembi yang dikirim ibu tirinya mencari tanaman di hutan.",
        "en": "One victim is Kumja Lembi, sent by her stepmother to collect herbs in the forest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khoirentak-tiger-c05",
      "source_id": "khoirentak-tiger-s1",
      "quote": "Another victim was Angom Nongban Kongyamba, who was seriously injured by the tiger during the later events. He later died from these injuries.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Angom Nongban Kongyamba meninggal akibat luka yang disebabkan harimau.",
        "en": "Angom Nongban Kongyamba dies of wounds caused by the tiger."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Misshaku Kongo adalah Guhyapada, penjaga Buddhis yang membawa vajra dan menampakkan gigi. Mulutnya terbuka untuk bunyi ha atau ah.",
      "en": "Misshaku Kongo is Guhyapada, a Buddhist guardian carrying a vajra and baring his teeth. His mouth opens to form the ha or ah sound.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "misshaku-kongo-c01",
        "misshaku-kongo-c02"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Di Jepang ia juga disebut Agyo karena bentuk mulut terbuka. Guhyapada dan Narayana dianggap sebagai manifestasi Vajrapani. Teks Zhu tian zhuan menyebut penjaga bernama Miji yang berikrar selalu berada dekat Buddha.",
      "en": "In Japan he is also called Agyo because of his open mouth. Guhyapada and Narayana are considered manifestations of Vajrapani. The Zhu tian zhuan names the guardian Miji who vows to remain near Buddha.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "misshaku-kongo-c01",
      "source_id": "misshaku-kongo-s1",
      "quote": "Japanese: Misshaku Kongō; Korean: Miljeok geumgang; Vietnamese: Mật tích kim cương ) is a symbol of overt violence: he wields a vajra mallet \"vajra-pāṇi\" (a diamond club, thunderbolt stick, or sun symbol) and bares his teeth.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Misshaku Kongo adalah Guhyapada, penjaga Buddhis yang membawa vajra dan menampakkan gigi.",
        "en": "Misshaku Kongo is Guhyapada, a Buddhist guardian carrying a vajra and baring his teeth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "misshaku-kongo-c02",
      "source_id": "misshaku-kongo-s1",
      "quote": "His mouth is depicted as being in the shape necessary to form the \"ha\" or \"ah\" sound.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mulutnya terbuka untuk bunyi ha atau ah.",
        "en": "His mouth opens to form the ha or ah sound."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "misshaku-kongo-c03",
      "source_id": "misshaku-kongo-s1",
      "quote": "Similarly, he is also known as Agyō (阿形, \"a\"-form, general term open-mouthed statues in aum pair) in Japan due to this detail as well.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Jepang ia juga disebut Agyo karena bentuk mulut terbuka.",
        "en": "In Japan he is also called Agyo because of his open mouth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "misshaku-kongo-c04",
      "source_id": "misshaku-kongo-s1",
      "quote": "Both Guhyapāda and Nārāyaṇa are seen as manifestations of Vajrapāni (Traditional Chinese: 執金剛神; simplified Chinese: 执金刚神; pinyin: Zhíjīngāng shén; Japanese: Shūkongōshin; Korean: Jip geumgang sin; Vietnamese: Chấp kim cang thần), with the name literally meaning \"vajra-wielding god\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guhyapada dan Narayana dianggap sebagai manifestasi Vajrapani.",
        "en": "Guhyapada and Narayana are considered manifestations of Vajrapani."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "misshaku-kongo-c05",
      "source_id": "misshaku-kongo-s2",
      "quote": "宝积经云。法意太子愿作力士。立名密迹。常亲近佛。在外威仪。省知如来一切密迹秘要之事。信乐不疑。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 11",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Zhu tian zhuan menyebut penjaga bernama Miji yang berikrar selalu berada dekat Buddha.",
        "en": "The Zhu tian zhuan names the guardian Miji who vows to remain near Buddha."
      }
    }

      ]

      "id": "nittaewo-c01",
      "source_id": "nittaewo-s1",
      "quote": "Nittaewo (or Nittevo) were said to be a small tribe of short-statured bigfoot or Yeti-like hominid cryptids native to Sri Lanka. No archeological evidence has been found to prove the existence of these humanoids, but they are ubiquitous in Veddha mythology and Sri Lankan folklore.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nittaewo adalah humanoid bertubuh kecil dalam mitologi Veddha dan cerita rakyat Sri Lanka.",

    {
      "id": "nittaewo-c02",
      "source_id": "nittaewo-s1",
      "quote": "No archeological evidence has been found to prove the existence of these humanoids, but they are ubiquitous in Veddha mythology and Sri Lankan folklore.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Belum ditemukan bukti arkeologis yang memastikan keberadaan mereka.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nittaewo-c03",
      "source_id": "nittaewo-s1",
      "quote": "They were covered in hair, which was often said to be reddish in colour, and were said to have very short, powerful arms with short, long-clawed hands.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka digambarkan berbulu dan memiliki tangan bercakar panjang.",
        "en": "They are described as hairy with long-clawed hands."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nittaewo-c04",
      "source_id": "nittaewo-s1",
      "quote": "Unlike monkeys, they walked upright always and had no tails.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dikatakan selalu berjalan tegak dan tidak berekor.",
        "en": "They are said to walk upright and lack tails."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nittaewo-c05",
      "source_id": "nittaewo-s1",
      "quote": "The nittaewo lived in small parties, and slept in caves or among the branches of trees, in leaf-nests of their own design.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerita menempatkan mereka dalam kelompok kecil yang tidur di gua atau sarang daun di pohon.",
        "en": "Tales place them in small groups sleeping in caves or leaf nests in trees."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nittaewo-c06",
      "source_id": "nittaewo-s1",
      "quote": "The Veddahs themselves were the enemies of the nittaewo, which had no defense against the Veddahs’ bows and arrows.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",

      "statement": {

      "source_id": "shinra-myojin-s1",
      "quote": "Shinra Myōjin (Japanese: 新羅明神; しんらみょうじん) is a Buddhist god associated with the Jimon branch of Tendai, a school of Japanese Buddhism that is chiefly worshiped in Onjō-ji. He is a god that was introduced to Japan from Korea (Silla) by incoming immigrants.",

      "source_id": "shinra-myojin-s1",
      "quote": "Historically, he was portrayed as a deity who arrived in Japan from Silla in order to protect Buddhist monks.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

      "source_id": "shinra-myojin-s1",
      "quote": "In art form, he is typically depicted as an elderly man dressed in the clothes in line with Tang-era officials with Buddhist elements, and historically, developed connections with a number of other figures, including Susanoo, Matarajin, and Mañjuśrī.",

      "quote": "The name \"Shinra Myōjin\" can be translated as the \"Shining deity of Silla\". Silla was a historical kingdom on the Korean Peninsula which existed between 57 BCE and 935.       "id": "Shomen Kongo adalah sosok yaksa yang menjadi objek pemujaan dalam Buddhisme Jepang. Ia dikenal sebagai pusat pemujaan kelompok Koshin dan dewa yang menahan sanshi.",
      "en": "Shomen Kongo is a yaksha figure worshipped in Japanese Buddhism. He is known as the focus of Koshin groups and a god restraining the sanshi.",

        "shomen-kongo-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut kepercayaan Tao, sanshi melaporkan perbuatan buruk seseorang kepada penguasa langit ketika orang itu tidur. Tradisi Koshin-machi menyuruh orang berjaga sepanjang malam Koshin. Kebiasaan berjaga bermula di kalangan bangsawan Heian dan kemudian meluas ke masyarakat.",
      "en": "In Taoist belief the sanshi report a person's misdeeds to the heavenly ruler while that person sleeps. The Koshin-machi custom requires people to stay awake throughout the Koshin night. The vigil custom begins among Heian aristocrats and later spreads among the public.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "shomen-kongo-c01",
      "source_id": "shomen-kongo-s1",
      "quote": "青面金剛（しょうめんこんごう）は、日本仏教における信仰対象の1つ。青面金剛明王とも呼ばれる。夜叉神である。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shomen-kongo-c02",
      "source_id": "shomen-kongo-s1",
      "quote": "庚申講の本尊として知られ、三尸（さんし）を押さえる神とされる。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikenal sebagai pusat pemujaan kelompok Koshin dan dewa yang menahan sanshi.",
        "en": "He is known as the focus of Koshin groups and a god restraining the sanshi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shomen-kongo-c03",
      "source_id": "shomen-kongo-s1",
      "quote": "道教では、人間の体内には三尸という3種類の悪い虫が棲み、人の睡眠中にその人の悪事をすべて天帝に報告に行くという。\nそのため、三尸が活動するとされる庚申の日（60日に一度）の夜は、眠ってはならないとされ、庚申の日の夜は人々が集まって、徹夜で過ごすという「庚申待」の風習があった。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kepercayaan Tao, sanshi melaporkan perbuatan buruk seseorang kepada penguasa langit ketika orang itu tidur.",
        "en": "In Taoist belief the sanshi report a person's misdeeds to the heavenly ruler while that person sleeps."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shomen-kongo-c04",
      "source_id": "shomen-kongo-s1",
      "quote": "庚申の日の夜は人々が集まって、徹夜で過ごすという「庚申待」の風習があった。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Koshin-machi menyuruh orang berjaga sepanjang malam Koshin.",
        "en": "The Koshin-machi custom requires people to stay awake throughout the Koshin night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shomen-kongo-c05",
      "source_id": "shomen-kongo-s1",
      "quote": "庚申待は平安貴族の間に始まり、近世に入ってからは、近隣の庚申講の人々が集まって夜通し酒宴を行うという風習が民間にも広まった。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kebiasaan berjaga bermula di kalangan bangsawan Heian dan kemudian meluas ke masyarakat.",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Suiten adalah dewa air dalam Buddhisme dan salah satu dari Dua Belas Dewa. Ia dipercaya tinggal di sebelah barat Gunung Sumeru.",
      "en": "Suiten is a water god in Buddhism and one of the Twelve Devas. He is believed to dwell west of Mount Sumeru.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "suiten-c01",
        "suiten-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia diyakini menguasai naga dan memiliki kemampuan mendatangkan hujan. Karena itu ia dipuja dalam ritual permohonan hujan. Kyoto National Museum menyimpan citra Suiten dari rangkaian Dua Belas Dewa yang dibuat pada 1127.",
      "en": "He is believed to rule dragons and possess the power to bring rain. He is therefore worshipped in rainmaking rites. Kyoto National Museum holds a Suiten image from a Twelve Devas series made in 1127.",

        "suiten-c03",
        "suiten-c04",

      ]

  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],

    {

      "url": "https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%B0%B4%E5%A4%A9",
      "title": "水天",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "suiten-c01",
      "source_id": "suiten-s1",
      "quote": "水天（すいてん）とは、仏教における天部の神で、水をつかさどる。須弥山の西方に住んでいるとされる。十二天のひとつである。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suiten adalah dewa air dalam Buddhisme dan salah satu dari Dua Belas Dewa.",
        "en": "Suiten is a water god in Buddhism and one of the Twelve Devas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suiten-c02",
      "source_id": "suiten-s1",
      "quote": "須弥山の西方に住んでいるとされる。十二天のひとつである。 概要",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dipercaya tinggal di sebelah barat Gunung Sumeru.",
        "en": "He is believed to dwell west of Mount Sumeru."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suiten-c03",
      "source_id": "suiten-s1",
      "quote": "水の神であり、竜を支配し、雨をもたらす力があるとされる。そのため雨乞いの儀式にも祭祀された。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia diyakini menguasai naga dan memiliki kemampuan mendatangkan hujan.",
        "en": "He is believed to rule dragons and possess the power to bring rain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suiten-c04",
      "source_id": "suiten-s1",
      "quote": "そのため雨乞いの儀式にも祭祀された。 もともとはインド・イランで語られていた古いアスラ族のヴァルナのことをさす",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena itu ia dipuja dalam ritual permohonan hujan.",
        "en": "He is therefore worshipped in rainmaking rites."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "suiten-c05",
      "source_id": "suiten-s1",
      "quote": "平安時代に、宮中の真言院で毎年正月に行なわれた修法に用いるため、1127年（大治2年）に制作された十二天画像のうちのひとつとして、仏画（絹本著色144.3 x 126.5cm）の水天像（京都国立博物館・所蔵。国宝）が残されている。ほかにも寺社に祀られる十二天のうちの一体としての作例は数多く見られた。日本においては、水や水天を象徴する生物として竜に限らず蛇があつかわれることもある。蛇王権現や水釈天などの項も参照。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kyoto National Museum menyimpan citra Suiten dari rangkaian Dua Belas Dewa yang dibuat pada 1127.",

      }
    }

      "source_id": "vadavagni-s1",
      "quote": "The Vadavagni is regarded to wander the seabed and consume its waters, awaiting the moment it could emerge and destroy the earth during the Pralaya, the dissolution of the earth at the end of an age. Various legends regarding the origin and suppression of the Vadavagni exist in Hindu literature, most prominently the descent of the goddess Saraswati as the Sarasvati river.",

    {

      "source_id": "vadavagni-s1",
      "quote": "The Vadavagni is described to emerge from the mouth of a mare called Vadavamukha (mare-face). This mare's mouth is sometimes described to be present under a sea at the South Pole.   "short_description": {
    "id": "Atavaka adalah yaksa dalam tradisi Buddhis yang dipandang sebagai Wisdom King dalam tradisi esoteris.",
    "en": "Atavaka is a yaksha in Buddhist tradition regarded as a Wisdom King in esoteric tradition.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "atavaka-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Atavaka adalah yaksa dalam tradisi Buddhis yang dipandang sebagai Wisdom King dalam tradisi esoteris. Dalam kisahnya, ia adalah yaksa pemakan manusia yang menghuni hutan Atavi.",
      "en": "Atavaka is a yaksha in Buddhist tradition regarded as a Wisdom King in esoteric tradition. In the tale he is a man-eating yaksha dwelling in the Atavi forest.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "atavaka-c01",
        "atavaka-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Vaisravana memberinya izin memangsa orang yang masuk ke bayangan tempat tinggalnya. Kekurangan tawanan membuat keluarga di wilayah itu dipaksa menyerahkan anak sebagai korban. Buddha memasuki kediamannya meski diperingatkan oleh penjaga pintu.",
      "en": "Vaisravana permits him to devour anyone entering the shadow of his dwelling. A shortage of prisoners forces households in the area to provide children as sacrifices. Buddha enters his dwelling despite the doorkeeper's warning.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "atavaka-c03",
        "atavaka-c04",
        "atavaka-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "atavaka-c01",
      "source_id": "atavaka-s1",
      "quote": "Āṭavaka (Sanskrit; Pali: Āḷavaka) is a popular figure in Buddhism. He is considered the most powerful earthly yakṣa at the time of the Buddha and regarded as a Wisdom King in esoteric tradition.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Atavaka adalah yaksa dalam tradisi Buddhis yang dipandang sebagai Wisdom King dalam tradisi esoteris.",
        "en": "Atavaka is a yaksha in Buddhist tradition regarded as a Wisdom King in esoteric tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atavaka-c02",
      "source_id": "atavaka-s1",
      "quote": "At the time of the Buddha, Āṭavaka was a man-eating yakṣa that lived deep in the forest of Āṭavī.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisahnya, ia adalah yaksa pemakan manusia yang menghuni hutan Atavi.",
        "en": "In the tale he is a man-eating yaksha dwelling in the Atavi forest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atavaka-c03",
      "source_id": "atavaka-s1",
      "quote": "The yakṣa was granted permission by King Vaiśravaṇa that he could seize and devour anyone who came within the shadow of his abode.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vaisravana memberinya izin memangsa orang yang masuk ke bayangan tempat tinggalnya.",
        "en": "Vaisravana permits him to devour anyone entering the shadow of his dwelling."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atavaka-c04",
      "source_id": "atavaka-s1",
      "quote": "due to a shortage of criminals, each household in the vicinity was forced to sacrifice one child to satiate the demon.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kekurangan tawanan membuat keluarga di wilayah itu dipaksa menyerahkan anak sebagai korban.",
        "en": "A shortage of prisoners forces households in the area to provide children as sacrifices."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "atavaka-c05",
      "source_id": "atavaka-s1",
      "quote": "Gadrabha warned the Buddha of the demon's wrathful nature, but the Buddha fearlessly entered Āṭavaka's abode and sat upon his throne.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Buddha memasuki kediamannya meski diperingatkan oleh penjaga pintu.",
        "en": "Buddha enters his dwelling despite the doorkeeper's warning."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Golden-Haired Hou adalah makhluk mitos China yang menjadi tunggangan bodhisattva Guanyin. Kepercayaan lain menganggapnya tahap perkembangan jiangshi, mayat hidup.",
      "en": "The Golden-Haired Hou is a Chinese mythical creature serving as bodhisattva Guanyin's mount. Another belief treats it as a stage of jiangshi, a reanimated corpse.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "golden-haired-hou-c01",
        "golden-haired-hou-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagian legenda menggambarkan Hou sebagai makhluk mirip anjing yang memakan otak naga. Dalam Journey to the West, ia adalah tunggangan Guanyin. Ia melarikan diri dan menjadi iblis Sai Taisui. Ia menculik Golden Sage Queen, sehingga raja jatuh sakit.",
      "en": "Some legends describe the Hou as doglike and feeding on dragon brains. In Journey to the West it is Guanyin's mount. It escapes and becomes the demon Sai Taisui. It abducts the Golden Sage Queen, causing the king to fall ill.",

        "golden-haired-hou-c03",

        "golden-haired-hou-c05",

      "id": "golden-haired-hou-c01",
      "source_id": "golden-haired-hou-s1",
      "quote": "The Golden-Haired Hou (Chinese: 金毛犼), is a mythical creature featured in Chinese mythology and legends. According to legends and literary works, the Golden-Haired Hou serves as the mount of the bodhisattva Guanyin.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Golden-Haired Hou adalah makhluk mitos China yang menjadi tunggangan bodhisattva Guanyin.",
        "en": "The Golden-Haired Hou is a Chinese mythical creature serving as bodhisattva Guanyin's mount."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "golden-haired-hou-c02",
      "source_id": "golden-haired-hou-s1",
      "quote": "Another folk belief identifies the Golden-Haired Hou (金毛犼) as a type of jiangshi (僵屍, \"hopping vampire\" or reanimated corpse) rather than a divine creature. According to this belief, a jiangshi first has white hair, which turns black after about 500 years. After another 500 years, its hair turns golden, and it becomes a Golden-Haired Hou.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepercayaan lain menganggapnya tahap perkembangan jiangshi, mayat hidup.",
        "en": "Another belief treats it as a stage of jiangshi, a reanimated corpse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "golden-haired-hou-c03",
      "source_id": "golden-haired-hou-s1",
      "quote": "Some legends describe the Hou as dog-like and as an enemy of dragons that feeds on their brains. Other accounts describe it as resembling a qilin (麒麟), an auspicious creature in Chinese mythology.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian legenda menggambarkan Hou sebagai makhluk mirip anjing yang memakan otak naga.",
        "en": "Some legends describe the Hou as doglike and feeding on dragon brains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "golden-haired-hou-c04",
      "source_id": "golden-haired-hou-s1",
      "quote": "In Journey to the West, the Golden-Haired Hou is the mount of Guanyin. He overhears that Mahamayuri has cursed a prince of the Kingdom of Purpuria (朱紫國) after the prince accidentally killed two of her followers while hunting. As punishment, the prince is made to suffer from lovesickness for three years.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Journey to the West, ia adalah tunggangan Guanyin.",
        "en": "In Journey to the West it is Guanyin's mount."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "golden-haired-hou-c05",
      "source_id": "golden-haired-hou-s1",
      "quote": "He became the demon Sai Taisui (賽太歲) and lived at Xiezhi Cave on Unicorn Mountain (麒麟山獬豸洞), where he commanded a group of demons.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melarikan diri dan menjadi iblis Sai Taisui.",
        "en": "It escapes and becomes the demon Sai Taisui."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "golden-haired-hou-c06",
      "source_id": "golden-haired-hou-s1",
      "quote": "He abducted the Golden Sage Queen (金聖皇后), one of the king's consorts, and forced her to live with him. The king became ill after her disappearance.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menculik Golden Sage Queen, sehingga raja jatuh sakit.",
        "en": "It abducts the Golden Sage Queen, causing the king to fall ill."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kangla sha adalah makhluk penjaga suci dalam mitologi Meitei, bertubuh singa dan berkepala naga bertanduk dua.",
    "en": "Kangla sha is a sacred guardian creature in Meitei mythology, with a lion's body and two-horned dragon head.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kangla-sha-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kangla sha adalah makhluk penjaga suci dalam mitologi Meitei, bertubuh singa dan berkepala naga bertanduk dua. Makhluk itu merupakan lambang kerajaan Meitei.",
      "en": "Kangla sha is a sacred guardian creature in Meitei mythology, with a lion's body and two-horned dragon head. The creature is a Meitei royal symbol.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kangla-sha-c01",
        "kangla-sha-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pemenang perlombaan tradisional ditentukan setelah menyentuh patungnya secara simbolis. Raja Nara Singh membangun kembali dua patung Kangla Sha pada 1844. Pasukan Inggris menghancurkan dua patung itu dengan tembakan artileri pada 20 Juli 1891.",
      "en": "Traditional race winners are declared after symbolically touching its statue. Raja Nara Singh reconstructs two Kangla Sha statues in 1844. British troops destroy the two statues with artillery fire on 20 July 1891.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kangla-sha-c03",
        "kangla-sha-c04",
        "kangla-sha-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

      "published": null,

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kangla-sha-c01",
      "source_id": "kangla-sha-s1",
      "quote": "In Meitei mythology and Sanamahism, the  indigenous religion of Manipur, Kanglā shā (Meitei: ꯀꯡꯂꯥ ꯁꯥ, lit. 'beast of the Kangla') is a sacred guardian beast with a lion's body and a two-horned dragon's head.\nIt is a royal symbol of the Meitei royalties (Ningthouja dynasty).\nThe most remarkable statues dedicated to \"Kangla Sa\" stand inside the Kangla.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kangla sha adalah makhluk penjaga suci dalam mitologi Meitei, bertubuh singa dan berkepala naga bertanduk dua.",
        "en": "Kangla sha is a sacred guardian creature in Meitei mythology, with a lion's body and two-horned dragon head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kangla-sha-c02",
      "source_id": "kangla-sha-s1",
      "quote": "It is a royal symbol of the Meitei royalties (Ningthouja dynasty).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk itu merupakan lambang kerajaan Meitei.",
        "en": "The creature is a Meitei royal symbol."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kangla-sha-c03",
      "source_id": "kangla-sha-s1",
      "quote": "In Meitei traditional race competitions, winners of the race are declared only after symbolically touching the statue of the dragon \"Kangla Sha\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemenang perlombaan tradisional ditentukan setelah menyentuh patungnya secara simbolis.",
        "en": "Traditional race winners are declared after symbolically touching its statue."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kangla-sha-c04",
      "source_id": "kangla-sha-s1",
      "quote": "During the months of June and July in the year 1844, Meitei king Raja Nara Singh (Meitei: ꯅꯤꯡꯊꯧ ꯅꯔꯁꯤꯡꯍ) reconstructed the two statues of the Kangla Sha dragons once again.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Raja Nara Singh membangun kembali dua patung Kangla Sha pada 1844.",
        "en": "Raja Nara Singh reconstructs two Kangla Sha statues in 1844."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kangla-sha-c05",
      "source_id": "kangla-sha-s1",
      "quote": "During the British conquest of the Anglo Manipur War of 1891, the British forces led by Captain Allen demolished the two statues of the Kangla Sha dragons by blasting them by artillery fire into pieces on 20 July 1891.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pasukan Inggris menghancurkan dua patung itu dengan tembakan artileri pada 20 Juli 1891.",
        "en": "British troops destroy the two statues with artillery fire on 20 July 1891."
      }
    }
  ],

Khyah dan Kawancha muncul dalam drama tari suci dan menjadi penjaga kuil. Tari Khyah Pyakhan pada festival Yenya menampilkan aktor berkostum Khyah.",

      "quote": "Khyāh (Newar: 𑐏𑑂𑐫𑐵𑑅; alternative spellings Khyā or Khyāk) is a mythical humanoid creature in Newari folklore. It is depicted as a fat, hairy and short ape-like creature.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Khyah adalah makhluk humanoid dalam cerita rakyat Newar, digambarkan pendek, gemuk, berbulu, dan mirip kera.",
        "en": "Khyah are humanoid creatures of Newar folklore, portrayed as short, fat, hairy and apelike."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khyah-legendary-creature-c02",
      "source_id": "khyah-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "Khyahs appear in children's stories popular in Newar society. A friendly Khyah fills the home with goodness while bad ones bring trouble. A white Khyah is believed to bring good luck while a black one can create problems. Encountering a Khyah can make one ill. Khyahs are said to tickle their victims to death.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Khyah yang bersahabat membawa kebaikan, sedangkan yang jahat membawa masalah.",
        "en": "Friendly khyah bring goodness, while bad ones bring trouble."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khyah-legendary-creature-c03",
      "source_id": "khyah-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "In Newar culture, Khyahs attend to Lakshmi, the goddess of wealth, and pictures of the deity show them guarding overflowing bags of coins. Household Khyahs usually dwell in the attic and dark storerooms. They are said to fear electric lighting.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kebudayaan Newar mereka mendampingi Lakshmi dan menjaga kantong uang dalam penggambarannya.",
        "en": "In Newar culture they attend Lakshmi and guard bags of coins in depictions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khyah-legendary-creature-c04",
      "source_id": "khyah-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "Household Khyahs usually dwell in the attic and dark storerooms. They are said to fear electric lighting.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Khyah rumah tinggal di loteng dan ruang penyimpanan gelap.",
        "en": "Household khyah dwell in attics and dark storerooms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khyah-legendary-creature-c05",
      "source_id": "khyah-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "The antithesis of the Khyah is the Kawanchā, a skeleton. Khyahs and Kawanchas appear as supporting characters in sacred dance dramas of the Newars. Images of Khyahs and Kawanchas are also placed at temples as guardians of the shrine.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Khyah dan Kawancha muncul dalam drama tari suci dan menjadi penjaga kuil.",
        "en": "Khyah and Kawancha appear in sacred dance dramas and as temple guardians."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "khyah-legendary-creature-c06",
      "source_id": "khyah-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "During the Yenya festival in Kathmandu, dance performances are held at market squares and the Durbar Square where actors dressed in Khyah costumes give dance performances. The dances, known as Khyāh Pyākhan (ख्याः प्याखं), consist of antics and tumbling.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tari Khyah Pyakhan pada festival Yenya menampilkan aktor berkostum Khyah.",
        "en": "Khyah Pyakhan dances at the Yenya festival feature actors in khyah costumes."
      }
    }
  ],
